## Description

#### Honcho shelving disposition — preservation only

The founder explicitly [shelved Honcho and cancelled all work](DIG-332.md#comment-e20ee74e-7e11-4966-ac1e-d461721f7565) at 2026-10-03 14:13:56 UTC, while requiring all completed, partially completed, in-progress and planned work to remain in the repository. This supersedes the earlier in-flight completion mandate and four-prerequisite permission for Honcho. Stop product implementation, design continuations, product validation and feature/cutover merges.

CEO is cancelling the 28 currently open Honcho project tasks, retaining done/cancelled history and all files/refs. The active DIG-343 owner has received the stop instruction; forced cancellation is board-only and must not be bypassed. This existing task is now only for shelving disposition and preservation receipts, not engineering completion.

##### DevGuru’s bounded responsibility

Reconcile cancellation and any queued/running Honcho continuation, keeping only preservation work active. Record every original as historically completed or cancelled/shelved with its actual partial state; never mark partial work accepted. Preserve all plans, findings and acceptance requirements for later resumption. Apply technical PR posture so no product PR can be merged inadvertently while shelved, retaining branches and exact reviewed/rejected heads. Do not delete refs or force-push. Documentation/archive changes may follow the existing preservation route without triggering releases.

Record the shelving decision, original ownership/status, current heads/PRs, known uncommitted work, unverified claims, defects and precise restart prerequisites in a `shelving-disposition` document. Pass that record to the existing source and archive owners. Include authored changes/notes from interrupted DIG-343 and the latest DIG-299 attempts. Verify any surviving execution/board restrictions; cancellation is not recovery clearance. Escalate only an actual surviving permission boundary.

This task completes its disposition when cancellation/PR posture and preservation inputs are accounted for. Final source byte/ref preservation and fresh-clone proof belong to [DIG-338](DIG-338.md); full portable records/artifacts belong to [DIG-337](DIG-337.md). Do not depend on those downstream tasks. They consume this disposition, preventing a cycle. No implementation acceptance or new product work is required for shelving.

Prominently supersede completion-boundary and decisions records while retaining historical revisions. Include the founder decision in the Honcho repo and canonical KB through the archive owners. Do not claim preservation complete before those owners verify it.

### Document `archive-handoff`: Honcho archive handoff without publication

Revision 1 (id `cfaeb544-54f7-40d6-b76d-808bd5197bd7`), updated 2026-10-03T07:13:19.997Z by DevGuru.

#### Honcho shutdown archive handoff — no publication required

DevGuru decision (DIG-261), 2026-10-03. This addresses CEO comment [b08caf34](DIG-333.md#comment-b08caf34-36fc-496c-a5aa-529b412b8218) on DIG-333. It is a technical repository handoff choice; no release, CI configuration, credential, production or default-branch change is authorized.

##### Decision and evidence

**Choose the existing non-default archive branch for Honcho's shutdown/resume handoff. Keep Honcho PR #26 unmerged and main unchanged for documentation delivery.** The cutoff expressly requires fresh-clone usability including non-default refs; it does not require merging every archive into main. This satisfies the repository-resident resume-guide boundary without introducing a publication action. DIG-335/DIG-337 still own final export, review and clone verification. This decision does not approve their currently incomplete deliverable.

At independently read main `d3b447a33488d385068525a81a06d39e84981dfd`, `.github/workflows/docker-build.yml:3` declares pushes to main and `v*` tags without a paths filter. The job declares package and attestation writes at `:17`; the registry is `ghcr.io` at `:11`; default-branch tagging selects `latest` at `:36`; registry login is conditional only on not being a pull request at `:38`; image push uses the same condition at `:50`; attestation is pushed at `:58`. Thus a documentation-only main push matches a configured publication path. I did not establish whether GitHub/Forgejo or any mirror currently executes it. The safe decision does not depend on assuming no runner or credentials exist. A prior test-only merge is not release authorization for the archive.

Independent `git ls-remote` and authenticated Forgejo metadata matched:

- `refs/heads/docs/paperclip-shutdown-2026-10-03` = `db11fbac7c8386581f5651eff7578027f9965e08`.
- [PR #26](https://repo.digikal.com/jawad/honcho/pulls/26) is open, WIP, unmerged, from that exact head to main.

This is the baseline archive head; DIG-337 must replace the final-head receipt after its final delta. No branch/default setting, workflow or PR field was changed in this analysis.

##### Clone and resume contract

The final delivery receipt must give the following explicit command, the then-final full SHA, and the guide path:

```bash
git clone --branch docs/paperclip-shutdown-2026-10-03 https://repo.digikal.com/jawad/honcho.git honcho-resume
cd honcho-resume
git rev-parse HEAD
cat docs/paperclip/shutdown-2026-10-03/README.md
```

A normal full clone can also expose the remote-tracking guide without switching source branches:

```bash
git show origin/docs/paperclip-shutdown-2026-10-03:docs/paperclip/shutdown-2026-10-03/README.md
```

No single-branch/default-branch-only clone is claimed to contain an omitted archive ref. The guide must retain exact commands to fetch every original work ref and any LFS/non-default attachment material. DIG-337 must independently clone the **final** branch, check its SHA and required content, run `sha256sum -c data/attachments.sha256` from its archive directory, and retain the per-repo output. Prior baseline clone results are historical and cannot substitute for that final receipt.

**Reversibility / Blast radius:** an existing archive branch requires no code, pipeline disable, tag, release or main push. It preserves all history and may later be merged through a separately reviewed publication-safe path. Reject editing shared CI/agent configuration during this analysis. Reject assuming a docs merge cannot publish. Reject asking the founder for a release solely to place documentation on main when the non-default clone path already satisfies the cutoff.

##### Applied coordination and remaining gates

This choice is recorded as an explicit acceptance constraint on DIG-333, which already blocks the final archive delta. KnowledgeCurator's existing DIG-335/DIG-337 owner path must carry the branch-specific clone command and leave Honcho PR #26 unmerged; TechLead receives no merge approval for it. The current branch/PR already match the selected posture, so no external mutation is necessary. DevGuru reviews the final archive against this constraint before the blocker can clear. Other repositories' publication paths require their own actual workflow review; this is not blanket main-merge permission for them.

CEO reviews this analysis with the completion-boundary document. There is no remaining founder publication question for this chosen Honcho handoff. If a later instruction specifically requires a main merge despite an executing publisher, that new requirement must identify the exact action and preserve founder-only publication approval. Existing recovery and credential questions remain independent.

##### Decision, scope and invariants

The founder-prohibited original To Do/backlog set is **DIG-172/104/171/109/79/236/208/228/227**. The thirteen Honcho plans in our register were blocked or in review at cutoff. Their subsequent freeze is a **DevGuru coordination safeguard**, not another founder instruction and not evidence that their code is unnecessary. All thirteen remain dormant unless an explicit scoped decision changes that posture. Completion of a prerequisite does not authorize their implementation.

**Invariants first:** original acceptance survives decomposition. An independent correction can be reviewed and merged into its approved target without declaring its parent or the rejected aggregate accepted. **Boundaries and ownership:** retain original authors, including the existing board-held QA runner and DevGuru documentation review. **Cost of your own time:** use existing source and existing handoffs; no new implementation backlog. **Reversibility:** preserve every branch and prior document revision; no migration, release, published player behavior, force-push or recovery bypass.

**Applied technical decision:** restore completion of the already-authored OPS-R1/R8 migration/dump corrections to their original author, ReleaseEngineer on DIG-313, alongside WH-1/2/3 and coordinated WH-4. The full stricter DIG-303/304 acceptance specifications remain binding contracts for those tools. Those two unstarted tasks stay dormant as preserved specifications; they must not start duplicate implementations. DIG-313 may submit separate bounded correction PRs for tools and webhook behavior, but is not done until every retained requirement is accepted. Remove only its DIG-303/304 scheduling edges, retaining DIG-299 and DIG-322 and all actual safety prerequisites. Exact applied response or owner-path denial belongs in the receipt document.

This reverses the earlier administrative split, not any correctness requirement. Reject leaving authored tool changes indefinitely orphaned behind two empty task branches. Reject calling WH-only acceptance completion of all authored DIG-313 work. Reuse is source provenance, not approval.

##### Evidence anchors and why the boundaries differ

All paths below are relative to the Honcho repository. Full heads are in the disposition register. Anchors identify immutable source inspected, not a later moving branch.

- **DIG-299 at `7ab3b94815f6c910fc9744325bf8188a757ce69b`:** `scripts/run_owned_rust_checks.py:57` constructs the child environment; `:149` validates command manifests; `:221` builds and copies the binary while holding the lock; `:252` writes owned-target identity; `:294` performs final process cleanup. `tests/owned_rust_target.py:169` verifies the cluster identifier and `:173` enforces the run-specific DB name. This is real runner implementation. It does not establish DIG-300's separate per-fixture failure/join contract. Its environment currently omits the downstream `DATABASE_URL` and webhook test alias, and the webhook fixture's name guard differs; adapting the contract must be completed by the original owners after recovery, not bypassed with a hand-built environment.
- **DIG-308 at `8e6bdd8e9e1ad5dca551b595f5e7275d8c4f8821`:** `rust/crates/honcho-core/src/repos/workspace.rs:356` assumes universal workspace position and `:360` deletes leases by field 2. Its session cascade at `repos/session.rs:700` instead uses queue-owned immutable `session_id` and excludes the current deletion key. Thus the missing DIG-302 typed ownership correction is real, but a new session grammar helper must preserve this stronger incarnation fence. Blind replacement with name-only predicates is unacceptable.
- **DIG-307 at `919b804511690f119939174595bad9f2f4b8ef98`:** `rust/crates/honcho-api/src/dialectic.rs:91` carries mutable name-based options; `:395` handles `finish_answer` before an enabled-tool check; `:422` dispatches returned calls; `:701` accepts that config in `execute_tool`; `:883` builds message filters from tool/caller names before search. These are actual consumption sites for DIG-306's immutable authorized recall scope and execution allowlist. Prompt improvements or the filter compiler alone cannot close this dependency. This is a source-grounded dependency assessment, not a completed exploit review.
- **DIG-313 at `47528cbb418a5dcb423e3c5d787efb9acd1a314b`:** `rust/crates/honcho/tests/webhook_delivery_cli.rs:20` requires an explicit DB, `:31` enforces its old test-name prefix, and `:39` runs native SQLx migrations. `rust/crates/honcho-core/src/repos/webhook.rs:167` begins a transaction, `:170` locks the workspace, and `:196` checks duplicates before the configured count. These webhook operations do not need the dump/handoff commands to prepare a fresh native database. Their safety and behavior still need actual guarded tests.
- **Existing tool source in the same DIG-313 head:** `scripts/rust_migration_handoff.py:49` maps shared migrations, `:56` separates the ledger, `:238` refuses tracked Rust-only reverse handoff, and `:357` groups DDL and bookkeeping. `scripts/rust_schema_drift.py:634` applies the same ledger SQL on the Alembic comparison side. `rust/scripts/dump_baseline_schema.sh:45` generates a DB name, `:47` still defaults output into repository migrations, and `:56` drops only after successful creation. These are useful authored corrections, not completed DIG-303/304 contracts: current-run cluster binding, scratch-only output, connection-free planning, independently pinned catalog assertions, full reverse-refusal and failure matrices remain required. Applying identical ledger SQL to both sides does not independently establish that the intended ledger schema is correct.
- **DIG-314 at `740b037a7cbd9940e1fc10543ec8a1fe3a2e0a95`:** `scripts/rust_openapi_route_diff.py` is the authored real-router inventory; its `qa-report` records seven mutation controls but missing schema controls, live assertions and repeatability proof. Original definitions in [DIG-102 verification](https://repo.digikal.com/jawad/infrastructure/src/branch/docs/paperclip-shutdown-2026-10-03/docs/paperclip/shutdown-2026-10-03/issues/DIG-102.md#document-findings-verification) distinguish narrow fatal stderr output (VAL-R1) from all of DIG-312's lifecycle work. DATA-F10 and QUEUE-Q10 require fail-closed fixtures and real manager/dream/summary assertions. They do not justify automatically launching all DIG-311/312 product fixes. A reproducible remaining product failure may establish a further prerequisite; it must be identified before adding scope.
- **DIG-315 at `92f660f2ae1bd7a1fe2686b260136f6309f908d9`:** `tests/rust_compat/test_validation_api_against_rust.py` contains only four promoted assertions. Its full `qa-report` enumerates the thirteen original cases: eleven metadata/name/message/query/required-field/filter cases, missing multipart, and PDF extraction. The PDF `test_pdf_file_processing` is a required case, not an optional aggregate feature. API-F09's additional range/null/enum/metadata cases also remain. The already-authored DIG-257 cutover correction is reusable; no PR #16 merge or new multipart project follows.

##### Concrete unresolved cutoff boundary

The smallest **currently proved** fresh implementation set is four original plans. This is not permission to run them and not a guarantee tests will discover no further defect:

1. **DIG-300 — deterministic fixture adapter (QAEngineer).** Implement its already-specified `tests/rust_process.py` and negative tests; adapt the named live/compat/startup/e2e/TypeScript fixture sites to the reviewed 299 manifest, allowlisted offline environment and bounded process cleanup. Needed by 260 and 314; no API product feature. Finish missing-binary/Bun, spawn/readiness/dead-DB/noisy-child/auth-mode failure controls. The existing 299 runner does not replace these fixture-specific assertions.
2. **DIG-302 — typed lease ownership (TechLead).** Implement the fixed task-kind/arity workspace ownership rules and collision/barrier tests from its nine-part handoff; integrate into 308 without replacing its immutable session incarnation/queue fence. Needed for 308 and therefore 309. No lease key/schema migration or broader worker graph.
3. **DIG-306 — immutable recall scope and allowed-tool dispatch (PlatformEngineer).** Implement its existing private scope/resolver, scope propagation and enabled-tool check before finish/dedup/dispatch, with real-router scripted private-canary/revocation/recreate/empty/>1000-membership tests. Needed for the changed 307 dialectic boundary; ordinary filters/prompts are insufficient. No new game/product feature, conclusion-scope expansion or native provider.
4. **DIG-258 — exact PDF parity case (TechLead original owner; retain actual issue ownership).** Recommended implementation is the preserved plan A: repair only a structurally identifiable legacy xref table on initial parse failure, bounded by the existing 5 MiB limit; reject ambiguous/encrypted/invalid or empty-text results and preserve valid-file handling. The cheapest disproof after authorization is the focused extractor test against the exact original bytes: if corrected offsets still yield no meaningful text, report that failure before expanding parser scope. Require the ordinary black-box HTTP 201 assertion. Needed for 315's thirteen-case requirement. Accepting a PDF residual instead would change the product acceptance contract and requires a real founder/product decision; this analysis does not choose that waiver.

**Recommendation to CEO:** review this narrow completion exception against the founder's shutdown instruction, rather than presenting the entire blocked Gate 4 graph for launch. Default is to finish these four exact prerequisites after valid recovery and their existing review gates, reuse authored 313 tools, and keep all other dormant plans and DIG-319 aggregate launch dormant. If the cutoff is interpreted as forbidding these four, request a concrete founder choice between this bounded exception and shutdown with the named originals explicitly incomplete. Do not ask the founder to decide the technical implementation, and do not describe incomplete preservation as acceptance. No new spend, credential grant, release, production change or irreversible action is part of the recommendation.

There is no evidence-based reason in this pass to make DIG-259/263/301 or the full DIG-311/312/320/321 scopes prerequisites of every independent correction. Their existing findings and plans remain unaccepted and preserved; they are still prerequisites of a future complete port where applicable. A future proof failure may establish a smaller additional correction, but no speculative blanket launch is authorized. DIG-303/304 are handled by source reuse above, not ignored.

##### Verification, migration and next action

This was a read-only scope analysis: issue descriptions, full current author ledgers/plans/comments, existing detailed handoffs, original finding definitions, and the specific source paths above were read. Remote refs were refreshed with `git ls-remote origin` for every started branch; ten are published and DIG-299 remains absent. No build, test, checkout edit, process recovery or repository mutation ran. Historical tests stay author claims; the scope proof is reading, not an adversarial approval of those branches.

No data migration occurs during shutdown under this decision. Existing schemas/ledger/checksums and old-consumer gates remain; only disposable owned targets may be used after the relevant gates clear. Any actual production migration or publication remains founder-only.

Original task command manifests and nine-part handoffs are the verification contracts. For every Rust invocation export `CARGO_TARGET_DIR="$PAPERCLIP_WORKSPACE_CWD/target"`, use one job and `flock /paperclip/.cargo-build.lock cargo ...`, run in the foreground, and retain exact source/binary/DB identities plus pass/fail/skip/xfail counts. No process may be left running when a heartbeat ends. No broad test suite is rerun merely for this analysis.

CEO reviews these decisions; original owners record application and preserve blockers. The existing recovery confirmation is [f0d4d6ef](DIG-327.md#interaction-f0d4d6ef-94a3-454c-93b5-9f08cd529413), independently read as pending; older cards are expired. DIG-299 ledger disappearance is not clearance. DIG-305 action `777cfc74-8a13-4def-81dd-e2216e869f1b` remains a board responsibility. DIG-336 is now CEO-approved/done, closing only freeze administration. DIG-264 is accepted and merged; DIG-333 remains blocked on the ten other unaccepted originals; no final shutdown claim follows.

##### Technical decision and execution boundary

Reversibility: close the five still-open product PRs (#4, #11, #24, #27, #29) without merging or deleting any branch. Keep PR #26 as the existing WIP archive review and require the documented explicit docs-branch clone; no main/default-branch/CI/publishing change. All merged history remains unchanged. Closing PRs is reversible and preserves discussion/diffs; leaving mergeable stale PRs open would invite an unauthorized continuation. DevGuru records the decision; TechLead, the established PR operator, applies it through one bounded preservation child. No repository code edit is authorized.

Cancellation bookkeeping belongs to CEO. The first read still contained 27 nonterminal product tasks plus newly cancelled DIG-343, so this version does not claim cancellation is complete. Project status is cancelled. DIG-299 and DIG-343 canonical live-run lists are empty. <a id="document-shutdown-disposition"></a>

### Document `shutdown-disposition`: Shutdown disposition and completion boundaries

Revision 9 (id `bc211c2a-c378-4c3a-b19a-9f19b7347020`), updated 2026-10-03T14:34:14.027Z by DevGuru.

> **SUPERSEDED — Honcho shelved on 2026-10-03T14:13:56.185Z.** The [founder stop](DIG-332.md#comment-e20ee74e-7e11-4966-ac1e-d461721f7565) cancels the completion/repair permissions and product implementation, validation and merges described below. These records remain historical evidence, not instructions to resume when blockers clear. The operative [shelving disposition](DIG-333.md#document-shelving-disposition) authorizes preservation only. Future product work needs a new founder unshelving decision.

#### Shutdown disposition — reconciled inventory and bounded completion

##### Operative authorization update — 2026-10-03 07:25 UTC

[CEO approved the revised analysis](DIG-333.md#comment-3be004ac-17a2-41bf-b988-1a80fbcda631) under the founder's existing in-flight completion instruction. **No new founder cutoff card is required.** DevGuru has applied the exact four technical dispositions to the original issues, with owner and independent acceptance gates retained:

| Original | Applied posture | Retained owner / actual gate |
|---|---|---|
| [DIG-300](https://repo.digikal.com/jawad/infrastructure/src/branch/docs/paperclip-shutdown-2026-10-03/docs/paperclip/shutdown-2026-10-03/issues/DIG-300.md) | Authorized deterministic fixture unit; blocked | QAEngineer; reviewed/integrated [DIG-299](DIG-299.md) after valid board recovery |
| [DIG-302](https://repo.digikal.com/jawad/infrastructure/src/branch/docs/paperclip-shutdown-2026-10-03/docs/paperclip/shutdown-2026-10-03/issues/DIG-302.md) | Authorized typed lease-ownership unit; blocked | TechLead; same [DIG-299](DIG-299.md) gate, preserving the downstream immutable-session fence |
| [DIG-306](https://repo.digikal.com/jawad/infrastructure/src/branch/docs/paperclip-shutdown-2026-10-03/docs/paperclip/shutdown-2026-10-03/issues/DIG-306.md) | Authorized immutable recall/allowed-tool unit; blocked | PlatformEngineer; same [DIG-299](DIG-299.md) gate |
| [DIG-258](https://repo.digikal.com/jawad/infrastructure/src/branch/docs/paperclip-shutdown-2026-10-03/docs/paperclip/shutdown-2026-10-03/issues/DIG-258.md) | Selected bounded repair plan A; backlog → blocked | TechLead; added [DIG-299](DIG-299.md) and [DIG-300](https://repo.digikal.com/jawad/infrastructure/src/branch/docs/paperclip-shutdown-2026-10-03/docs/paperclip/shutdown-2026-10-03/issues/DIG-300.md) as first-class safety prerequisites |

This supersedes the earlier precautionary freeze **only for those four exact units**. Source, spike, build and runtime work waits until relevant prerequisites are independently accepted and integrated; the approval alone is not recovery clearance or code acceptance. Failed focused disproof returns to DevGuru for smallest-scope judgment; it does not authorize a parser rewrite, acceptance waiver or whole graph. No new task graph was created.

The other nine plans in the thirteen-plan inventory remain dormant: [DIG-259](https://repo.digikal.com/jawad/infrastructure/src/branch/docs/paperclip-shutdown-2026-10-03/docs/paperclip/shutdown-2026-10-03/issues/DIG-259.md), [DIG-263](https://repo.digikal.com/jawad/infrastructure/src/branch/docs/paperclip-shutdown-2026-10-03/docs/paperclip/shutdown-2026-10-03/issues/DIG-263.md), [DIG-301](https://repo.digikal.com/jawad/infrastructure/src/branch/docs/paperclip-shutdown-2026-10-03/docs/paperclip/shutdown-2026-10-03/issues/DIG-301.md), [DIG-303](https://repo.digikal.com/jawad/infrastructure/src/branch/docs/paperclip-shutdown-2026-10-03/docs/paperclip/shutdown-2026-10-03/issues/DIG-303.md), [DIG-304](https://repo.digikal.com/jawad/infrastructure/src/branch/docs/paperclip-shutdown-2026-10-03/docs/paperclip/shutdown-2026-10-03/issues/DIG-304.md), [DIG-311](https://repo.digikal.com/jawad/infrastructure/src/branch/docs/paperclip-shutdown-2026-10-03/docs/paperclip/shutdown-2026-10-03/issues/DIG-311.md), [DIG-312](https://repo.digikal.com/jawad/infrastructure/src/branch/docs/paperclip-shutdown-2026-10-03/docs/paperclip/shutdown-2026-10-03/issues/DIG-312.md), [DIG-320](https://repo.digikal.com/jawad/infrastructure/src/branch/docs/paperclip-shutdown-2026-10-03/docs/paperclip/shutdown-2026-10-03/issues/DIG-320.md), [DIG-321](https://repo.digikal.com/jawad/infrastructure/src/branch/docs/paperclip-shutdown-2026-10-03/docs/paperclip/shutdown-2026-10-03/issues/DIG-321.md). The separate nine founder-prohibited To Do/backlog items remain untouched. Historical source-started classifications and prior freeze receipts remain evidence at their original dates, not current authorization.

The ten unaccepted original blockers on this task remain. The [archive handoff](DIG-333.md#document-archive-handoff) is unchanged: explicit clone of Honcho's existing docs branch, final SHA/clone/hash proof, PR #26 unmerged. Its owner has been sent the concrete constraint through the existing [DIG-335](DIG-335.md)/[DIG-337](DIG-337.md) path. No publisher, repository or recovery mutation was made in this disposition heartbeat. Application/readback and owner acknowledgement receipts are in [decisions](DIG-333.md#document-decisions).

The previous proposal/exception language below is historical and superseded by this authorization; the technical boundaries, disproof conditions and original acceptance remain binding.


Evidence read 2026-10-03 during run `0f9e2956-d00d-429c-a89d-fca78e8a6951`. This is a technical shutdown record, **not a review verdict or completion certificate**. CEO reviews this analysis; DevGuru remains responsible for unfinished technical disposition.

##### Current operative update — 2026-10-03 07:03 UTC

The [completion-boundary analysis](DIG-333.md#document-completion-boundary) responds to both CEO wake comments and supersedes the first pass's overbroad cutoff interpretation. The original founder-prohibited set is nine To Do/backlog tasks (DIG-172/104/171/109/79/236/208/228/227). The thirteen Honcho plans below were blocked/in_review at cutoff; their freeze is our coordination safeguard, not a new founder instruction. They remain dormant while exact necessary completion work is resolved.

##### Decision and invariants
The [frozen cutoff](DIG-332.md#document-shutdown-cutoff) controls. Eleven original issues contain started source changes. Thirteen are unstarted plans or placeholder branches; four are coordination records, one is a founder-only host plan, and DIG-327 is the existing recovery waiting path. No todo/backlog product task is authorized for implementation. No full Gate 4 launch or cutover merge is authorized.
**Invariants first:** a pushed ref establishes preservation only; acceptance requires the original bounded criteria, actual final-head validation and independent review. **Boundaries and ownership:** retain original authors and the existing QA/DevGuru board recovery assignments. **Blast radius:** never import the rejected aggregate Rust port through a small correction PR. **Reversibility:** preserve pushed history and dirty work; no reset, deletion, force-push, schema deployment, credential or production action.
